Compare controlled food-solution models

Science Atlas360° · VisionariesAI Labs Private Limited · CL-0101

Aim and prediction

Compare matched pure-solute preparations; <model> & "probe" are separate from real-food measurements.

Apparatus and procedure

  1. Write an aim and predict the ordering of controlled model solutions. These are pure-solute analogues,not actual food measurements.
  2. Prepare equal nominal volumes and record the four independent formal concentrations. Keep the pure-water control.
  3. Raise the ideal virtual probe above the rim before selecting another cell. Move the carriage,then lower until the complete bulb is immersed.
  4. Record the selected sample and its virtual readout. A raised probe has no pH reading. All five calculated predictions are shown separately from this single probe observation.
  5. Repeat for the other samples. Compare equal formal concentrations to explore acid strength,and compare changed concentrations while retaining other conditions.
  6. Apply formulas for amount,mass,speciation and pH. Explain hydrogen-ion ratios on the logarithmic scale. Write a result,conclusion and model limitations.

Student result and conclusion

At matched formal concentrations the acid models differ. A raised probe has no readout; all five predictions remain separate. <result> & "limits".

Precautions and model limitations

  • Ideal aqueous25°C pure-solute models use concentration-based equilibrium constants. Real food mixtures contain other acids,salts,buffers and variable composition. No model pH is certified for a food product.
  • The nonconsuming virtual probe has negligible displacement and carryover. It does not model an electrode response time,junction potential,drift,calibration uncertainty or fouling. Full bulb immersion gates the displayed ideal readout.
  • Bicarbonate is a closed aqueous formal-carbon system with sodium charge balance and negligible gas headspace/exchange. CO₂* is the combined dissolved CO₂ and carbonic-acid pool. Its apparent first dissociation constant is not the constant of pure H₂CO₃.
  • Nominal prepared-solution density is1g/mL. Solute and water masses are formal preparation accounts;they do not separately count every water/hydronium molecule after proton redistribution. The single aqueous contents mass remains unchanged.
  • Protonation state fractions are mathematical populations,not visible molecular counts or calibrated colour. Calculated pH values and hydrogen-ion ratios do not measure titratable acidity,nutritional value or health/tooth safety.
  • The120-second guide stages carriage travel,insertion,comparison and withdrawal. It is an explanation sequence,not experimental kinetics.
